<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let $`\mathop{\mathrm{IMM}}\nolimits _{n,n}`$ be the $`(1,1)`$ entry of a product of <i>n</i> independent $`n\times n`$ matrices of variables. Over every field of characteristic zero, every syntactically homogeneous $`\Sigma\Pi\Sigma\Pi\Sigma`$ circuit computing $`\mathop{\mathrm{IMM}}\nolimits _{n,n}`$ has at least $`n^{\sqrt n/400}`$ gates for all sufficiently large <i>n</i>, with an absolute threshold independent of the field. Bottom linear forms may have arbitrary support, and arbitrary finite fan-in, fan-out, and gate sharing are allowed. Over every field, a block expansion gives such circuits with at most $`n^{\sqrt n+4}`$ gates for <i>n</i> ≥ 2. Thus the gate complexity over characteristic-zero fields is $`n^{\Theta(\sqrt n)}`$.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist absolute constants $c>0$ and $n_0$ such that, for every integer $n\geq n_0$, every syntactically homogeneous $\Sigma\Pi\Sigma\Pi\Sigma$ circuit over $\mathbb C$ computing $\operatorname{IMM}_{n,n}$ has size at least $$n^{c\sqrt n}.$$ One may take $c=1/400$.
  - Explanation: Gives a superpolynomial size lower bound for homogeneous arithmetic circuits computing balanced iterated matrix multiplication.
- **C2** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-parameters)): For all sufficiently large $n$, the parameters in <a href="#eq:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameters">[eq:parameters]</a> satisfy $$\begin{gathered} \frac{\sqrt n}{2}\le s\le\sqrt n,\qquad \lambda\ge\frac38,\qquad 0<\rho<1,\qquad v,u=\Theta(n^3),\qquad a,b=\Theta(n^{5/2}),\\ a\ge k,\qquad 2n\le\frac12\min(a,b),\qquad n^{-1/2}\le\alpha=n^{-1/2}\exp(O(1/a)),\qquad \alpha^{-1}\le\sqrt n,\\ \alpha^\rho\le\beta\le\alpha^\rho\exp(C/b), \qquad \beta\le\frac2{\sqrt n},\\ q=n^{-1/2}\exp\bigl(O(s\log n/n+1/a)\bigr), \qquad n^{-1/2}\le q\le\frac2{\sqrt n} \le n^{-2/5},\qquad q\le\frac12, \end{gathered}$$ where $C$ is an absolute constant.
  - Explanation: Balances differentiation and multiplication while controlling the errors introduced by rounding the source degrees.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-circuit)): There are absolute constants $C>0$ and $n_0$ with the following property. For $n\ge n_0$, use the parameters in <a href="#eq:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameters">[eq:parameters]</a> and any partition of the matrix layers with $k$ layers in $V$ and $m$ layers in $U$. Let $K$ be a field, and let $g\in K[V,U]$ be a homogeneous degree-$n$ polynomial computed by a syntactically homogeneous $\Sigma\Pi\Sigma\Pi\Sigma$ circuit of size $S$. Then $$R(g)\le 2S^2D\exp(C\sqrt n)\,n^{-\sqrt n/100}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the rank measure of a homogeneous circuit using its size and the source dimension.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-product-rank)): Let $K$ be a field, and let $Q_1,\ldots,Q_r\in K[V,U]$ be homogeneous of respective positive degrees $e_1,\ldots,e_r$, with $\sum_{j=1}^r e_j=n$. Then $$R\left(\prod_{j=1}^r Q_j\right) \le 2D\prod_{j=1}^r H_{e_j}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds a product by factoring each bidegree contribution through a smaller homogeneous space.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-degree-estimates)): For every integer $e\ge1$, $$\label{eq:nearest-integer-bound} H_e\le q^{d_e}\bigl(1+4q^{1-2d_e}\bigr)\le5.$$ If $1\le e<t_0$, then $$\label{eq:low-degree-distances} d_e= \begin{cases} se/(2n),&e\text{ even},\\ 1/2-se/(2n),&e\text{ odd}, \end{cases} \qquad d_e\ge\frac{se}{2n}.$$ In this range, odd degrees satisfy $H_e\le q^{d_e/2}$, whereas even degrees satisfy $$\label{eq:even-degree-bound} H_e\le q^{d_e}\exp\bigl(4q^{1-se/n}\bigr).$$ There is an absolute constant $C_1$ such that, for every finite list of positive integers $e_1,\ldots,e_r$ with $\sum_j e_j\le n$, $$\label{eq:even-corrections} \sum_{\substack{j:\ e_j<t_0\\ e_j\text{ even}}} q^{1-se_j/n}\le C_1\sqrt n.$$
  - Explanation: Controls the discrepancy penalties of factor degrees, retaining the aggregate saving from small even degrees.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-imm)): Over $\mathbb C$, for the partition <a href="#eq:balanced-schedule" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:balanced-schedule">[eq:balanced-schedule]</a>, there is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$R(\operatorname{IMM}_{n,n})\ge D\exp(-C\sqrt n).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the rank of iterated matrix multiplication using its path structure and trace moments.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-occupation-moments)): Let $M=(M_1,\ldots,M_r)$ be uniform among the weak compositions of an integer $t\ge0$ into $r\ge1$ parts. For integers $d_i\ge0$ and $H=\sum_i d_i$, $$\mathbb E\prod_i(M_i)_{d_i} =\frac{(t)_H}{r^{\overline H}}\prod_i d_i!. \label{eq:composition-moment}$$ The right side is zero when $H>t$.* *Let $Z_1,\ldots,Z_r$ be independent geometric variables on the nonnegative integers with mean $G=t/r$. If a polynomial $P$ has nonnegative coefficients in the basis $\{\prod_i(z_i)_{d_i}\}$, then $$\mathbb E P(M)\le\mathbb E P(Z). \label{eq:occupation-upper}$$ If $t>0$ and all terms of that expansion have total order at most $H_0\le t/2$, then also $$\mathbb E P(M) \ge \exp\left(-\frac{H_0^2}{t}-\frac{H_0^2}{2r}\right) \mathbb E P(Z). \label{eq:occupation-lower}$$
  - Explanation: Computes exact factorial moments of uniform weak compositions and compares nonnegative factorial expansions with independent geometric occupations.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-word-sum)): For all sufficiently large $n$, use the layer schedule <a href="#eq:balanced-schedule" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:balanced-schedule">[eq:balanced-schedule]</a> and the parameters $\alpha,\beta,\rho$ of <a href="#eq:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameters">[eq:parameters]</a>. For a word $\tau\in\{\mathcal N,\mathcal D\}^n$, let $w(\tau)$ be its number of switches, let $d_V(\tau),d_U(\tau)$ count its $\mathcal D$ positions in the respective variable groups, and let $N_V(\tau)$ be its set of $\mathcal N$ positions in group $V$. For $t\in N_V(\tau)$, let $h_t$ be the number of internal vertices adjacent to layer $t$ whose two incident layers both have type $\mathcal N$. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\label{eq:word-sum} \sum_{\tau\in\{\mathcal N,\mathcal D\}^n} n^{-w(\tau)}\alpha^{-d_V(\tau)}\beta^{d_U(\tau)} \prod_{t\in N_V(\tau)}\bigl(1+\alpha^{-1}n^{-h_t}\bigr) \le \exp(C\sqrt n).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the pairing-word sum through isolated-position removal and balanced interval discrepancies.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-characteristic-zero)): There is an absolute integer $n_0$ such that, for every field $K$ of characteristic zero and every $n\ge n_0$, every syntactically homogeneous $\Sigma\Pi\Sigma\Pi\Sigma$ circuit over $K$ computing $\operatorname{IMM}_{n,n}$ has at least $n^{\sqrt n/400}$ gates. Bottom fan-in and bottom support are unrestricted.
  - Explanation: Transfers the gate lower bound to characteristic-zero fields by preserving the nonvanishing of integer minors.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-upper)): Let $K$ be any field and $n\ge2$. Put $$t=\lceil\sqrt n\rceil, \qquad r=\left\lceil\frac nt\right\rceil.$$ There is a syntactically homogeneous $\Sigma\Pi\Sigma\Pi\Sigma$ circuit over $K$ computing $\operatorname{IMM}_{n,n}$ whose number of gates, including leaves, is at most $$\label{eq:upper-gate-bound} 2n^3+rn^2+rn^{t+1}+n^{r-1}+1 \le n^{\sqrt n+4}.$$
  - Explanation: Constructs a homogeneous arithmetic circuit by sharing entries of consecutive matrix blocks.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Iterated matrix multiplication** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Define $$\label{eq:imm-definition} \operatorname{IMM}_{w,d} =\bigl(X^{(1)}\cdots X^{(d)}\bigr)_{1,1} =\sum_{i_1,\ldots,i_{d-1}\in[w]} x^{(1)}_{1,i_1}x^{(2)}_{i_1,i_2}\cdots x^{(d)}_{i_{d-1},1}.$$
- **Arithmetic circuit** ([source](#pm-h-3)): An arithmetic circuit over a field $K$ is a finite directed acyclic graph.
- **Five-layer circuit** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A $\Sigma\Pi\Sigma\Pi\Sigma$ circuit has five computational layers, listed from output to leaves, with respective gate types $+,\times,+,\times,+$ and a single output gate.
- **Syntactic homogeneity** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The circuit is *syntactically homogeneous* if it has the following formal-degree assignment: variables have degree $1$, constants have degree $0$, product gates add their input degrees with multiplicity, and all inputs of a sum gate have the same degree, which is assigned to that gate.
- **Circuit size** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We define *size* to be the number of vertices, including leaves.
- **Homogeneous polynomial space** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a variable set $E$ and an integer $t\ge0$, write $\mathcal H_E(t)=K[E]_t$ for the vector space of homogeneous polynomials of degree $t$.
- **Differentiation–multiplication operator** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For $Q\in K[V,U]$, let $$T_Q=Q(\partial_V,U)$$ be the operator on $K[V,U]$ obtained by replacing each variable in $V$ by its formal partial derivative and each variable in $U$ by multiplication by that variable.
- **Finite rank map** ([source](#eq:rank-map)): Define the finite-dimensional map $$\label{eq:rank-map} F_g=g_{[k,m]}(\partial_V,U): \mathcal H_V(a)\otimes\mathcal H_U(b) \longrightarrow \mathcal H_V(a-k)\otimes\mathcal H_U(b+m), \qquad R(g)=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}F_g.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-parameters): Monotonicity and logarithmic derivative estimates for ceiling errors. -> Balanced parameters with controlled rounding losses.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-product-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-rank): Apply positive-discrepancy factors first and bound intermediate homogeneous dimensions. -> Product rank bound.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-degree-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-estimates): Nearest-integer estimates and convexity control the aggregate even-degree errors. -> Degree-dependent penalties.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-circuit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-estimates-2): Decompose circuit terms and expand one high-degree factor occurrence when needed. -> Circuit rank upper bound.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-occupation-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-occupation-moments): Extract coefficients of factorial-moment generating functions and compare moment ratios. -> Exact moments and bounded-loss comparison.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-word-sum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-word-sum): Remove isolated pairing positions and count runs using balanced interval discrepancies. -> Pairing-word sum bound.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-prop-imm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-word-sum-2): Combine trace moment estimates with the positive-semidefinite trace inequality. -> IMM rank lower bound.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-word-sum-3): Compare upper and lower rank bounds and absorb the exponential loss at sufficiently large degree. -> Complex-field circuit size lower bound.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-cor-characteristic-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-characteristic-zero): Preserve nonzero integer minors under characteristic-zero field embeddings. -> Characteristic-zero gate lower bound.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-prop-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-upper): Expand consecutive matrix blocks and share block-entry gates. -> Matching-scale homogeneous circuit construction.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: The characteristic-zero lower bound requires syntactic homogeneity and the stated arithmetic layer pattern, while bottom fan-in and support remain unrestricted.
- **I2**: The block construction computes the formal iterated matrix multiplication polynomial with shared block entries and a gate count including leaves.
- **I3**: Circuit size includes leaves and is distinct from computational-gate count and wire size.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A1**: Treat the lower bound as a numerical matrix-kernel runtime estimate. -> Abstract formal-polynomial gates do not specify numerical execution cost.
- **A2**: Apply the lower bound over positive-characteristic fields. -> Integer minors can vanish after reduction, so the field transfer does not apply.
- **A3**: Claim the asymptotic lower bound from small constructed examples. -> The required absolute threshold is unspecified.
- **A4**: Read a gate-size bound as a wire-size bound. -> Repeated input occurrences and shared gates have different counting conventions.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The block construction is finite and explicit. Its polynomial identity and gate accounting can be checked exactly; finite checks do not certify the asymptotic lower bound.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON integer n.
# PRECONDITIONS: 2 <= n <= 5; commuting distinct matrix-entry variables over the integers.
# OUTPUTS: JSON sparse polynomial coefficients, gate count including leaves, and coarse gate envelope.
# TIME: O(n^(2*n)) conservative exact-operation bound on the admitted domain.
# SPACE: O(n^(2*n)) conservative bound on stored monomials.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer coefficients using python-flint fmpz; no floating-point operations.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises ValueError; unavailable exact arithmetic raises ImportError.
import sys, json, math, itertools
from flint import fmpz
n = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
if type(n) is not int or not 2 <= n <= 5:
    raise ValueError('outside admitted domain')
t = math.isqrt(n)
if t*t < n:
    t += 1
lengths = [min(t, n-start) for start in range(0, n, t)]
r = len(lengths)
blocks = []
offset = 0
for ell in lengths:
    entries = {}
    for left in range(n):
        for right in range(n):
            poly = {}
            for middle in itertools.product(range(n), repeat=ell-1):
                vertices = (left,) + middle + (right,)
                monomial = tuple((offset+j)*n*n + vertices[j]*n + vertices[j+1] for j in range(ell))
                poly[monomial] = poly.get(monomial, fmpz(0)) + fmpz(1)
            entries[left, right] = poly
    blocks.append(entries)
    offset += ell
result = {}
for joints in itertools.product(range(n), repeat=r-1):
    endpoints = (0,) + joints + (0,)
    contribution = {(): fmpz(1)}
    for j in range(r):
        updated = {}
        for mon1, c1 in contribution.items():
            for mon2, c2 in blocks[j][endpoints[j], endpoints[j+1]].items():
                key = mon1 + mon2
                updated[key] = updated.get(key, fmpz(0)) + c1*c2
        contribution = updated
    for mon, coefficient in contribution.items():
        result[mon] = result.get(mon, fmpz(0)) + coefficient
count = 2*n**3 + r*n**2 + sum(n**(ell+1) for ell in lengths) + n**(r-1) + 1
envelope = 2*n**3 + r*n**2 + r*n**(t+1) + n**(r-1) + 1
assert count <= envelope
assert all(len(mon) == n for mon in result)
print(json.dumps({'terms': [[list(mon), int(coefficient)] for mon, coefficient in sorted(result.items())], 'gates': count, 'envelope': envelope}, sort_keys=True))
```

    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON integer n.\n# PRECONDITIONS: 2 <= n <= 5; commuting distinct matrix-entry variables over the integers.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON sparse polynomial coefficients, gate count including leaves, and coarse gate envelope.\n# TIME: O(n^(2*n)) conservative exact-operation bound on the admitted domain.\n# SPACE: O(n^(2*n)) conservative bound on stored monomials.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer coefficients using python-flint fmpz; no floating-point operations.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises ValueError; unavailable exact arithmetic raises ImportError.\nimport sys, json, math, itertools\nfrom flint import fmpz\nn = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\nif type(n) is not int or not 2 <= n <= 5:\n    raise ValueError('outside admitted domain')\nt = math.isqrt(n)\nif t*t < n:\n    t += 1\nlengths = [min(t, n-start) for start in range(0, n, t)]\nr = len(lengths)\nblocks = []\noffset = 0\nfor ell in lengths:\n    entries = {}\n    for left in range(n):\n        for right in range(n):\n            poly = {}\n            for middle in itertools.product(range(n), repeat=ell-1):\n                vertices = (left,) + middle + (right,)\n                monomial = tuple((offset+j)*n*n + vertices[j]*n + vertices[j+1] for j in range(ell))\n                poly[monomial] = poly.get(monomial, fmpz(0)) + fmpz(1)\n            entries[left, right] = poly\n    blocks.append(entries)\n    offset += ell\nresult = {}\nfor joints in itertools.product(range(n), repeat=r-1):\n    endpoints = (0,) + joints + (0,)\n    contribution = {(): fmpz(1)}\n    for j in range(r):\n        updated = {}\n        for mon1, c1 in contribution.items():\n            for mon2, c2 in blocks[j][endpoints[j], endpoints[j+1]].items():\n                key = mon1 + mon2\n                updated[key] = updated.get(key, fmpz(0)) + c1*c2\n        contribution = updated\n    for mon, coefficient in contribution.items():\n        result[mon] = result.get(mon, fmpz(0)) + coefficient\ncount = 2*n**3 + r*n**2 + sum(n**(ell+1) for ell in lengths) + n**(r-1) + 1\nenvelope = 2*n**3 + r*n**2 + r*n**(t+1) + n**(r-1) + 1\nassert count <= envelope\nassert all(len(mon) == n for mon in result)\nprint(json.dumps({'terms': [[list(mon), int(coefficient)] for mon, coefficient in sorted(result.items())], 'gates': count, 'envelope': envelope}, sort_keys=True))\n",
    "test_vectors": [

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The formal circuit model can guide symbolic implementations that compare shared circuit representations under a fixed depth and homogeneity constraint. This software use is inferred; no software runtime bound is supplied. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The target is a formal polynomial and the result measures arithmetic circuit gates. No game dynamics or simulation accuracy contract is provided. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A symbolic circuit inspection tool could enforce the formal-degree and gate-count conventions before reporting the stated bounds. The paper supplies mathematical conditions rather than a tool implementation. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The block construction explicitly expands matrix-product entries into monomials and shares the resulting entry gates. It gives a constructive arithmetic-circuit size envelope, while the lower bound applies only to the restricted circuit model. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Shared gates are permitted in an arithmetic circuit graph, but the paper establishes no storage layout or data-structure operation bounds. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The gates perform formal arithmetic over fields and the size convention counts mathematical vertices. Physical area, timing and memory traffic are outside this model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A lower bound for this formal polynomial in a restricted arithmetic model is not a security assumption or an adversarial protocol analysis. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns exact formal polynomial computation and syntactic homogeneity. It supplies no learning objective, training procedure or statistical guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The gate-size comparison is an algebraic complexity result. It does not specify an optimization instance, feasible region or operational scheduling guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The computational object is a finite arithmetic circuit graph with gate sharing. No communication or fault model is part of its gate count. |
| other (algebraic_circuit_complexity) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | Algebraic circuit complexity is the direct subject: a characteristic-zero lower bound and an any-field block construction delimit the gate complexity of homogeneous iterated matrix multiplication at the stated depth. |

## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The lower bound is stated over fields of characteristic zero. Blocked use: ['Using the lower bound over a field of positive characteristic.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The lower bound requires syntactic homogeneity, including the formal degrees retained by zero gates. Blocked use: ['Applying the lower bound to a circuit with homogeneous output but without the required formal-degree assignment.']
- **L3** (formal_scope): The lower bound concerns the stated arithmetic layer pattern. Blocked use: ['Using the lower bound for unrestricted-depth circuits or a different computational layer pattern.']
- **L4** (formal_scope): The lower bound targets $\operatorname{IMM}_{n,n}$ with the balanced width and degree. Blocked use: ['Substituting a different polynomial or independently varying width and degree without another bound.']
- **L5** (unknown): The lower bound has an absolute threshold $n_0$ whose numerical value is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Treating an arbitrary small instance as covered by the asymptotic lower bound.']
- **L6** (nonconstructive): The final lower-bound comparison increases the absolute threshold without supplying a numerical threshold. Blocked use: ['Using the asymptotic comparison as a finite-instance certificate without checking all parameter hypotheses.']
- **L7** (formal_scope): Size, computational gates and wire occurrences are distinct quantities in this model. Blocked use: ['Interpreting the size bound as a numerical runtime, a wire count or a physical hardware resource estimate.']
- **L8** (formal_scope): The computations concern exact formal polynomials. Blocked use: ['Using the lower bound as a claim about approximate numerical matrix multiplication or floating-point error.']
- **L9** (edge_case): The finite upper construction is stated over any field in its specified input range; it does not extend the lower bound to positive characteristic. Blocked use: ['Applying the upper gate-count envelope outside its stated input range.', 'Inferring a positive-characteristic lower bound from the all-field upper construction.']
- **L10** (formal_scope): The full substituted operator respects products, but the finite rank map includes a bidegree projection. Blocked use: ['Treating the finite projected map as multiplicative without decomposing the factors into bidegree components.']
- **L11** (formal_scope): The occupation comparison is termwise and requires the stated sign and order conditions; it does not assert independence of composition coordinates. Blocked use: ['Replacing uniform weak compositions by independent geometric variables as an exact joint distribution.', 'Applying moment comparisons to arbitrary signed polynomial expansions.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): Gate sharing and unrestricted bottom support are permitted rather than excluded by the lower-bound model. Blocked use: ['Rejecting a circuit merely because it shares subcircuits or has broad bottom linear forms.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 135 (`lean/docs/135.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Homogeneous depth-five lower bounds for iterated matrix multiplication](../../preprints/Homogeneous-depth-five-lower-bounds-for-iterated-matrix-multiplication-September-25-2026/Homogeneous-depth-five-lower-bounds-for-iterated-matrix-multiplication-September-25-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Let $\mathrm{IMM}_{n,n}$ be the $(1,1)$ entry of the product of $n$ independent $n\times n$ variable matrices. The formalization proves that, over every characteristic-zero field, syntactically homogeneous depth-five $\Sigma\Pi\Sigma\Pi\Sigma$ circuits computing this polynomial require at least $n^{\sqrt n/400}$ gates for all sufficiently large $n$, with a threshold independent of the field. Arbitrary bottom support, finite fan-in and fan-out, and sharing are allowed.
>
> It also gives, over every field and for $n\ge2$, circuits with at most $n^{\sqrt n+4}$ gates. These are the lower and upper bounds selected from the paper.

Comparator source `DepthFive.lean` (4970 bytes, sha256 `f9c478730e4609f5ae2ad51ffac1e21f55c1391f41290664af7fe9eb4e7c1196`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open scoped BigOperators
universe u

namespace Problem335

inductive D5Leaf (K : Type u) (n : ℕ) where
  | scalar : K → D5Leaf K n
  | variable : (Fin n × Fin n × Fin n) → D5Leaf K n

def d5LeafDegree {K : Type u} {n : ℕ} : D5Leaf K n → ℕ
  | D5Leaf.scalar _ => 0
  | D5Leaf.variable _ => 1

structure Depth5Circuit (K : Type u) [CommSemiring K] (n : ℕ) where
  leafCount : ℕ
  leaves : Fin leafCount → D5Leaf K n
  bottomCount : ℕ
  bottomInputs : Fin bottomCount → List (K × Fin leafCount)
  bottomDegree : Fin bottomCount → ℕ
  bottomHomogeneous :
    ∀ i entry, entry ∈ bottomInputs i → d5LeafDegree (leaves entry.2) = bottomDegree i
  bottomEmpty :
    ∀ i, bottomInputs i = [] → bottomDegree i = 0
  lowerCount : ℕ
  lowerInputs : Fin lowerCount → List (Fin bottomCount)
  middleCount : ℕ
  middleInputs : Fin middleCount → List (K × Fin lowerCount)
  middleDegree : Fin middleCount → ℕ
  middleHomogeneous :
    ∀ i entry, entry ∈ middleInputs i →
      ((lowerInputs entry.2).map bottomDegree).sum = middleDegree i
  middleEmpty :
    ∀ i, middleInputs i = [] → middleDegree i = 0
  upperCount : ℕ
  upperInputs : Fin upperCount → List (Fin middleCount)
  outputInputs : List (K × Fin upperCount)
  outputDegree : ℕ
  outputHomogeneous :
    ∀ entry, entry ∈ outputInputs →
      ((upperInputs entry.2).map middleDegree).sum = outputDegree
  outputEmpty :
    outputInputs = [] → outputDegree = 0

def d5LeafValue {K : Type u} [CommSemiring K] {n : ℕ}
    (x : D5Leaf K n) : MvPolynomial (Fin n × Fin n × Fin n) K :=
  match x with
  | D5Leaf.scalar a => MvPolynomial.C a
  | D5Leaf.variable i => MvPolynomial.X i

def bottomValue {K : Type u} [CommSemiring K] {n : ℕ}
    (c : Depth5Circuit K n) (i : Fin c.bottomCount) :
    MvPolynomial (Fin n × Fin n × Fin n) K := by
  classical
  exact ((c.bottomInputs i).map (fun entry =>
    MvPolynomial.C entry.1 * d5LeafValue (c.leaves entry.2))).sum

def lowerValue {K : Type u} [CommSemiring K] {n : ℕ}
    (c : Depth5Circuit K n) (i : Fin c.lowerCount) :
    MvPolynomial (Fin n × Fin n × Fin n) K := by
  classical
  exact ((c.lowerInputs i).map (fun j => bottomValue c j)).prod

def middleValue {K : Type u} [CommSemiring K] {n : ℕ}
    (c : Depth5Circuit K n) (i : Fin c.middleCount) :
    MvPolynomial (Fin n × Fin n × Fin n) K := by
  classical
  exact ((c.middleInputs i).map (fun entry =>
    MvPolynomial.C entry.1 * lowerValue c entry.2)).sum

def upperValue {K : Type u} [CommSemiring K] {n : ℕ}
    (c : Depth5Circuit K n) (i : Fin c.upperCount) :
    MvPolynomial (Fin n × Fin n × Fin n) K := by
  classical
  exact ((c.upperInputs i).map (fun j => middleValue c j)).prod

def circuitValue {K : Type u} [CommSemiring K] {n : ℕ}
    (c : Depth5Circuit K n) : MvPolynomial (Fin n × Fin n × Fin n) K := by
  classical
  exact (c.outputInputs.map (fun entry =>
    MvPolynomial.C entry.1 * upperValue c entry.2)).sum

def circuitSize {K : Type u} [CommSemiring K] {n : ℕ}
    (c : Depth5Circuit K n) : ℕ :=
  c.leafCount + c.bottomCount + c.lowerCount + c.middleCount + c.upperCount + 1

def immLayer (K : Type u) [CommSemiring K] (n : ℕ) (t : Fin n) :
    Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) (MvPolynomial (Fin n × Fin n × Fin n) K) :=
  fun i j => MvPolynomial.X (t, i, j)

def imm (K : Type u) [CommSemiring K] (n : ℕ) :
    MvPolynomial (Fin n × Fin n × Fin n) K := by
  classical
  by_cases h : 0 < n
  · let matrices :
        List (Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n)
          (MvPolynomial (Fin n × Fin n × Fin n) K)) :=
      List.ofFn (fun t : Fin n => immLayer K n t)
    exact matrices.prod ⟨0, h⟩ ⟨0, h⟩
  · exact 0

def ceilSqrt (n : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  Nat.sqrt n + if (Nat.sqrt n) ^ 2 = n then 0 else 1

def ceilDiv (n t : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  (n + t - 1) / t

def upperGateBound (n : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  let t := ceilSqrt n
  let r := ceilDiv n t
  2 * n ^ 3 + r * n ^ 2 + r * n ^ (t + 1) + n ^ (r - 1) + 1

theorem main :
    (∃ threshold : ℕ, ∀ size : ℕ, threshold ≤ size →
      ∀ circuit : Depth5Circuit ℂ size, circuitValue circuit = imm ℂ size →
        (size : ℝ) ^ (Real.sqrt (size : ℝ) / 400) ≤ (circuitSize circuit : ℝ)) ∧
    (∃ threshold : ℕ, ∀ (FieldType : Type u) [Field FieldType] [CharZero FieldType],
      ∀ size : ℕ, threshold ≤ size → ∀ circuit : Depth5Circuit FieldType size,
        circuitValue circuit = imm FieldType size →
          (size : ℝ) ^ (Real.sqrt (size : ℝ) / 400) ≤ (circuitSize circuit : ℝ)) ∧
    (∀ (FieldType : Type u) [Field FieldType] (size : ℕ), 2 ≤ size →
      ∃ circuit : Depth5Circuit FieldType size, circuitValue circuit = imm FieldType size ∧
        circuitSize circuit ≤ upperGateBound size ∧
        (upperGateBound size : ℝ) ≤ (size : ℝ) ^ (Real.sqrt (size : ℝ) + 4)) := by
  sorry

end Problem335

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let $`\mathop{\mathrm{IMM}}\nolimits _{n,n}`$ be the $`(1,1)`$ entry of a product of <i>n</i> independent $`n\times n`$ matrices of variables. Over every field of characteristic zero, every syntactically homogeneous $`\Sigma\Pi\Sigma\Pi\Sigma`$ circuit computing $`\mathop{\mathrm{IMM}}\nolimits _{n,n}`$ has at least $`n^{\sqrt n/400}`$ gates for all sufficiently large <i>n</i>, with an absolute threshold independent of the field. Bottom linear forms may have arbitrary support, and arbitrary finite fan-in, fan-out, and gate sharing are allowed. Over every field, a block expansion gives such circuits with at most $`n^{\sqrt n+4}`$ gates for <i>n</i> ≥ 2. Thus the gate complexity over characteristic-zero fields is $`n^{\Theta(\sqrt n)}`$.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
